//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/40px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="44" data-file-height="31" /></a></span></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs expansion</b>&#32;with: <a href="/wiki/2023_British_cabinet_reshuffle" title="2023 British cabinet reshuffle">2023 British cabinet reshuffle</a>, as well as Raab resignation over bullying probe and promotion of Dowden to Deputy Prime Minister in April 2023. You can help by <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rishi_Sunak&amp;action=edit&amp;section=">adding to it</a>.  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2023</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sunak_ministry" title="Sunak ministry">Sunak ministry</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg/220px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg/330px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg/440px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_chairing_the_first_meeting_of_his_Cabinet.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5463" data-file-height="3642" /></a><figcaption>Sunak chairing the first meeting of his cabinet</figcaption></figure>
<p>Sunak began to appoint his cabinet on 25 October 2022. <a href="/wiki/Jeremy_Hunt" title="Jeremy Hunt">Jeremy Hunt</a> remained as chancellor, a role he was given during the <a href="/wiki/Truss_ministry" title="Truss ministry">Truss ministry</a> after <a href="/wiki/Kwasi_Kwarteng" title="Kwasi Kwarteng">Kwasi Kwarteng</a> was dismissed on 14 October. <a href="/wiki/Dominic_Raab" title="Dominic Raab">Dominic Raab</a> was also re-appointed Deputy Prime Minister and Justice Secretary, a role he was given during the premiership of Boris Johnson. <a href="/wiki/James_Cleverly" title="James Cleverly">James Cleverly</a> remained as <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Secretary" title="Foreign Secretary">Foreign Secretary</a> with <a href="/wiki/Suella_Braverman" title="Suella Braverman">Suella Braverman</a> returning as <a href="/wiki/Home_Secretary" title="Home Secretary">Secretary of State for the Home Department</a>, a role from which she had previously resigned during the Truss ministry. <a href="/wiki/Ben_Wallace_(politician)" title="Ben Wallace (politician)">Ben Wallace</a> remained as <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Defence" title="Secretary of State for Defence">Secretary of State for Defence</a>, a role from which he had previously during the Johnson and Truss ministries. <a href="/wiki/Michael_Gove" title="Michael Gove">Michael Gove</a> returned as <a href="/wiki/Levelling_Up_Secretary" class="mw-redirect" title="Levelling Up Secretary">Levelling Up Secretary</a>, a role he was sacked from by Johnson, and <a href="/wiki/Grant_Shapps" title="Grant Shapps">Grant Shapps</a> was demoted from Home Secretary to <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Business,_Energy_and_Industrial_Strategy" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretary of State for Business, Energy and Industrial Strategy">Secretary of State for Business, Energy and Industrial Strategy</a>. <a href="/wiki/Penny_Mordaunt" title="Penny Mordaunt">Penny Mordaunt</a> remained as Leader of the House of Commons and Lord President of the council, roles from which she had during the Truss ministry.
</p><p>Other key appointments included <a href="/wiki/Simon_Hart" title="Simon Hart">Simon Hart</a> as <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_Secretary_to_the_Treasury" title="Parliamentary Secretary to the Treasury">Parliamentary Secretary to the Treasury</a> and <a href="/wiki/Chief_Whip" title="Chief Whip">Chief Whip</a> of the House of Commons, <a href="/wiki/Nadhim_Zahawi" title="Nadhim Zahawi">Nadhim Zahawi</a> as party chairman, <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Dowden" title="Oliver Dowden">Oliver Dowden</a> as <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Duchy_of_Lancaster" title="Chancellor of the Duchy of Lancaster">Chancellor of the Duchy of Lancaster</a>, <a href="/wiki/Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_Coffey" title="Thérèse Coffey">Thérèse Coffey</a> as <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Environment,_Food_and_Rural_Affairs" title="Secretary of State for Environment, Food and Rural Affairs">Environment Secretary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mel_Stride" title="Mel Stride">Mel Stride</a> as <a href="/wiki/Work_and_Pensions_Secretary" class="mw-redirect" title="Work and Pensions Secretary">Work and Pensions Secretary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mark_Harper" title="Mark Harper">Mark Harper</a> as Transport Secretary.
</p><p>Sunak was criticised for his appointment of <a href="/wiki/Gavin_Williamson" title="Gavin Williamson">Gavin Williamson</a> and Dominic Raab to the cabinet. Both had been accused of bullying, a charge they both denied.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Williamson later resigned, whilst eight complaints were being formally investigated over the allegations brought against Dominic Raab. Sunak was also criticised for returning Braverman to the cabinet, despite her previous resignation for breaking the <a href="/wiki/Ministerial_Code" title="Ministerial Code">Ministerial Code</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Sunak said that his appointments to the government were in an attempt to reflect a "unified party".<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> On 29 January 2023 Sunak dismissed Zahawi, stating that ethics adviser <a href="/wiki/Laurie_Magnus_(executive)" title="Laurie Magnus (executive)">Laurie Magnus</a> had found a "serious breach" of the ministerial code. Sunak's judgement was questioned for originally reappointing Zahawi, and there were also questions over whether Sunak should have dismissed him earlier.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Raab resigned as Deputy Prime Minister and Justice Secretary in April 2023 after the report into bullying allegations criticised his "intimidating" behaviour.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Sunak appointed Oliver Dowden as Deputy Prime Minister and <a href="/wiki/Alex_Chalk" title="Alex Chalk">Alex Chalk</a> as Justice Secretary.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg/220px-Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="243" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg/330px-Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg/440px-Prime_Minister_Sunak_delivered_remarks_at_the_COP_27_Summit.jpg 2x" data-file-width="508" data-file-height="561" /></a><figcaption>Sunak delivering a speech at <a href="/wiki/COP27" class="mw-redirect" title="COP27">COP27</a> in Egypt.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In a reversal of his predecessor's policy, Sunak reinstated the ban on <a href="/wiki/Fracking_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Fracking in the United Kingdom">fracking</a> on 26 October as outlined in the 2019 Conservative manifesto.<sup id="cite_ref-RSF_5_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSF_5-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October, Sunak initially said that he would not attend the <a href="/wiki/2022_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference" title="2022 United Nations Climate Change Conference">2022 United Nations Climate Change Conference</a> in Egypt, to allow him to concentrate on urgent domestic matters.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Following pressure from MPs, environmentalist campaigners and others, Sunak announced that he would attend.<sup id="cite_ref-:7_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sunak attended a reception held by the king at Buckingham Palace on 4 November.<sup id="cite_ref-RSKC_1_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSKC_1-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Sunak told the meeting of approximately 200 politicians and campaigners that the UK would continue with its environmental aims after the end of its <a href="/wiki/2021_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference" title="2021 United Nations Climate Change Conference">COP26</a> presidency.<sup id="cite_ref-RSKC_1_126-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSKC_1-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> In his speech, Sunak said that climate change would cause long-lasting human suffering, and that because of inaction, people risked giving their children a desperate inheritance. Sunak also paid tribute to the king's longstanding work for the environment.<sup id="cite_ref-RSKC_1_126-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSKC_1-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 7 November at the <a href="/wiki/2022_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference" title="2022 United Nations Climate Change Conference">COP27</a> summit, Sunak launched The Forest and Climate Leaders' Partnership (FCLP), building on a policy called the <a href="/wiki/Glasgow_Climate_Pact" title="Glasgow Climate Pact">Glasgow Climate Pact</a>, originally started at COP 26.<sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_1_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_1-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_2_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_2-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The partnership aims to halt and reverse deforestation by 2030, bringing 26 countries and the European Union together.<sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_1_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_1-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> These countries account for 60% of <a href="/wiki/Global_GDP" class="mw-redirect" title="Global GDP">global GDP</a> and over 33% of the world's forests and together with private funding, the partnership has total funds of $23.8bn.<sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_1_127-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_1-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_2_128-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_2-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> In his speech to the Forest and Climate Leaders' Summit, Sunak said that the world's forests have been undervalued and underestimated, yet were one of the natural wonders of the world.<sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_1_127-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_1-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> He then asked attendees to build upon what had already been achieved to secure an incredible legacy for generations to come.<sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_1_127-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_1-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> The FCLP will hold annual meetings and starting in 2023, it will publish an annual Global Progress Report that includes independent assessments.<sup id="cite_ref-RSDF_1_127-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSDF_1-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy">Foreign policy</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_(52907950657).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_%2852907950657%29.jpg/220px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_%2852907950657%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_%2852907950657%29.jpg/330px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_%2852907950657%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_%2852907950657%29.jpg/440px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_attends_G7_Summit_in_Hiroshima_Japan_%2852907950657%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7020" data-file-height="4682" /></a><figcaption>Sunak with Joe Biden, <a href="/wiki/Olaf_Scholz" title="Olaf Scholz">Olaf Scholz</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ursula_von_der_Leyen" title="Ursula von der Leyen">Ursula von der Leyen</a> at the <a href="/wiki/49th_G7_summit" title="49th G7 summit">49th G7 summit</a> in Hiroshima, Japan</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_(52850509197).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_%2852850509197%29.jpg/220px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_%2852850509197%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_%2852850509197%29.jpg/330px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_%2852850509197%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_%2852850509197%29.jpg/440px-Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_meets_Italian_PM_Giorgia_Meloni_%2852850509197%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7140" data-file-height="4762" /></a><figcaption>Sunak meets Italian Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Giorgia_Meloni" title="Giorgia Meloni">Giorgia Meloni</a> in London</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/List_of_international_prime_ministerial_trips_made_by_Rishi_Sunak" title="List of international prime ministerial trips made by Rishi Sunak">List of international prime ministerial trips made by Rishi Sunak</a> and <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Rishi_Sunak#Foreign_policy" title="Premiership of Rishi Sunak">Premiership of Rishi Sunak §&#160;Foreign policy</a></div>
<p>Following the <a href="/wiki/2022_missile_explosion_in_Poland" title="2022 missile explosion in Poland">2022 missile explosion in Poland</a>, Sunak met US president <a href="/wiki/Joe_Biden" title="Joe Biden">Joe Biden</a> and gave a speech about the explosion.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> He later met Ukrainian president <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy" title="Volodymyr Zelenskyy">Volodymyr Zelenskyy</a> during his first visit to <a href="/wiki/Kyiv" title="Kyiv">Kyiv</a>, and pledged to give Ukraine £50&#160;million in aid.<sup id="cite_ref-:9_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_positions">Political positions</span></h2>
<p>Sunak has been described as a moderate within his party with a <a href="/wiki/Technocratic" class="mw-redirect" title="Technocratic">technocratic</a> or managerial leadership style.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Euronews" title="Euronews">Euronews</a>, Sunak is "frequently perceived as a pragmatist and as belonging to the centre-ground of the Conservative Party".<sup id="cite_ref-:42_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:42-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> He opposed <a href="/wiki/Trussonomics" class="mw-redirect" title="Trussonomics">Trussonomics</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-:42_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:42-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> and although described as a fellow <a href="/wiki/Thatcherite" class="mw-redirect" title="Thatcherite">Thatcherite</a>, he is viewed as less <a href="/wiki/Economically_liberal" class="mw-redirect" title="Economically liberal">economically liberal</a> than <a href="/wiki/Liz_Truss" title="Liz Truss">Liz Truss</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 2023, Sunak's perception as a centrist has been contrasted with descriptions of his government's policies on transgender and migration issues as being <a href="/wiki/Socially_conservative" class="mw-redirect" title="Socially conservative">socially conservative</a>, with Jessica Elgot of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Guardian" title="The Guardian">The Guardian</a></i> describing Sunak as the "most socially conservative PM of his generation".<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Robert Shrimsley of the <i><a href="/wiki/Financial_Times" title="Financial Times">Financial Times</a></i> described Sunak as someone whose "easy manner, career in global finance and ethnic background might suggest a more cosmopolitan conservative", even though he is socially conservative and pragmatic.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Meanwhile, the <i><a href="/wiki/New_Statesman" title="New Statesman">New Statesman</a></i> described Sunak as uneasily straddling both <a href="/wiki/Liberal-conservative" class="mw-redirect" title="Liberal-conservative">liberal-conservative</a> and <a href="/wiki/National-conservative" class="mw-redirect" title="National-conservative">national-conservative</a> instincts.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> In July 2023, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Economist" title="The Economist">The Economist</a></i> described him as "the most right-wing Conservative prime minister since <a href="/wiki/Margaret_Thatcher" title="Margaret Thatcher">Margaret Thatcher</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Crime_and_anti-terror_strategy">Crime and anti-terror strategy</span></h3>
<p>On crime, Sunak proposed an automatic one-year extension to prison sentences for prolific criminals, as well as cutting the minimum sentence before a foreign criminal is eligible for deportation from twelve months to six.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> In August 2022, he proposed life imprisonment for leaders of child grooming gangs, and for police to record the ethnicity of those involved in such gangs.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> He also proposed widening the <a href="/wiki/Prevent_strategy" class="mw-redirect" title="Prevent strategy">Prevent strategy</a> by widening the definition of "extremism".<sup id="cite_ref-:8_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="European_Union">European Union</span></h3>
<p>Sunak supported the <a href="/wiki/Leave_campaign" class="mw-redirect" title="Leave campaign">Leave campaign</a> during the <a href="/wiki/2016_United_Kingdom_European_Union_(EU)_membership_referendum" class="mw-redirect" title="2016 United Kingdom European Union (EU) membership referendum">2016 United Kingdom European Union (EU) membership referendum</a>. Speaking in 2022, Sunak said: "I voted for <a href="/wiki/Brexit" title="Brexit">Brexit</a>, I believe in Brexit."<sup id="cite_ref-:11_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:11-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> Sunak also said the UK would not be pursuing a relationship with the EU post-Brexit if the UK had to align with <a href="/wiki/EU_laws" class="mw-redirect" title="EU laws">EU laws</a>. In January 2023, Sunak confirmed intentions to remove EU legislation from the UK statute book that year, saying that it should be a "collective effort".<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:11_141-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:11-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> In February 2023, Sunak negotiated a proposed agreement with the EU on <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland" title="Northern Ireland">Northern Ireland</a>'s trading arrangements which was published as the "<a href="/wiki/Windsor_Framework" title="Windsor Framework">Windsor Framework</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> On 27 February, Sunak delivered a statement to the House of Commons, saying that the proposed agreement "protects Northern Ireland's place in our Union.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> On 22 March, the date of the parliamentary vote, 22 Conservative MPs and six DUP MPs voted against the government legislation.<sup id="cite_ref-:0d_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0d-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The vote ultimately passed by 515 votes to 29.<sup id="cite_ref-:0d_146-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0d-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy_and_the_environment">Energy and the environment</span></h3>
<p>Sunak signed the Conservative Environment Pledge (CEP), as shown on the Conservative Environment Network (CEN) website which has the support of approximately 127 MPs.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCP_1_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCP_1-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-RSCP_2_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCP_2-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> The CEP's five main commitments are using Brexit freedoms for the environment and sustainable farming, backing British clean energy suppliers to boost <a href="/wiki/Energy_security" title="Energy security">energy security</a>, encouraging the use of domestic insulation and electric vehicle charging points, implementing the <a href="/wiki/Environment_Act_2021" title="Environment Act 2021">Environment Act</a>, and backing technologies that will help to achieve clean growth.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCP_1_147-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCP_1-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> During the leadership contest held over summer 2022, Sunak told the CEN that he was engaged with the protection of the environment for future generations.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCP_3_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCP_3-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Sunak has said he is committed to keeping the legal commitment of reaching <a href="/wiki/Net_zero" class="mw-redirect" title="Net zero">net zero</a> by 2050.<sup id="cite_ref-RSNZ_1_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSNZ_1-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> During the summer, he said that he intended to make the UK energy independent by 2045,<sup id="cite_ref-RSNZ_2_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSNZ_2-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> while advocating for more offshore <a href="/wiki/Wind_power_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Wind power in the United Kingdom">windpower</a>, more <a href="/wiki/Solar_power_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Solar power in the United Kingdom">solar panels</a> on rooftops and improved insulation of homes to make them more <a href="/wiki/Energy_efficiency_in_British_housing" title="Energy efficiency in British housing">energy efficient</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-RSNZ_2_151-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSNZ_2-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> Sunak is said to have listened to fellow MPs with a green agenda and that he was a believer in net zero for the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-RSNZ_4_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSNZ_4-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Sunak also voted against a call for Britain to eliminate most <a href="/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions_by_the_United_Kingdom" title="Greenhouse gas emissions by the United Kingdom">greenhouse gas emissions</a> from transportation by 2030.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_133-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup></p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png/220px-Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png/330px-Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png/440px-Chancellor_hails_global_cooperation_as_landmark_tax_reforms_aimed_for_2023.png 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="640" /></a><figcaption>Sunak hailing global cooperation in October 2021, after 136 countries agreed a new system ensuring large multi-nationals pay the right tax in the right places</figcaption></figure><p>While campaigning in August 2022, Sunak wrote that he would restrict the use of solar panels on farmland but would make sure solar is installed on commercial buildings, properties and sheds,<sup id="cite_ref-RSSO_1_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSSO_1-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> saying "on my watch, we will not lose swathes of our best farmland to solar farms."<sup id="cite_ref-RSSO_1_153-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSSO_1-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> The trade association Solar Energy U.K. said the solar industry was "deeply concerned" with both candidates intentions.<sup id="cite_ref-RSSO_2_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSSO_2-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sunak has backed <a href="/wiki/Fracking_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Fracking in the United Kingdom">fracking</a>, where it is supported by local residents.<sup id="cite_ref-RSF_1_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSF_1-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> On 19 October in the debate on "Ban on Fracking for Shale Gas Bill (Division 66)", he voted with the government against the ban on fracking.<sup id="cite_ref-RSF_2_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSF_2-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> Fracking had been banned by the government in November 2019 after a report by the <a href="/wiki/North_Sea_Transition_Authority" title="North Sea Transition Authority">Oil and Gas Authority</a> found that it was not possible at that time to predict the probability or strength of earthquakes caused by fracking.<sup id="cite_ref-RSF_3_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSF_3-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-RSF_4_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSF_4-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While chancellor, Sunak attended <a href="/wiki/2021_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference" title="2021 United Nations Climate Change Conference">COP26</a> in Glasgow.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCO_1_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCO_1-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> During the speech he gave on 3 November, he said that he felt optimism despite daunting challenges and that by bringing together finance ministers, businesses and investors, COP 26 could begin to deliver targets from the <a href="/wiki/Paris_Agreement" title="Paris Agreement">Paris Agreement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCO_2_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCO_2-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> He outlined three actions: First, the need for increased public investment, with the UK committing £100 million to the Taskforce on Access to Climate Finance. He announced support for a new Capital Markets Mechanism which will issue <a href="/wiki/Green_bond" title="Green bond">green bonds</a> in the UK to fund renewable energy in developing countries.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCO_2_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCO_2-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Second, mobilising private finance, with the Glasgow Financial Alliance for Net Zero bringing together organisations with assets over $130&#160;trillion to be deployed.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCO_2_160-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCO_2-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Third, the rewiring of the entire global financial system for net zero, which would include better climate data, mandatory sustainability disclosures, climate risk surveillance and stronger global reporting standards.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCO_2_160-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCO_2-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Also announced was that the UK will become the first ever 'Net Zero Aligned Financial Centre'.<sup id="cite_ref-RSCO_2_160-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSCO_2-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During an interview in July 2022, Sunak said that wind generation would be a part of his governments' energy policies, but he wanted to reassure communities that there would not be a relaxation of the current onshore planning laws, with more of a focus on offshore wind farms.<sup id="cite_ref-RSWP_1_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSWP_1-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> This stance was confirmed by the PM's press team in October, who said that Sunak wants "offshore not onshore wind".<sup id="cite_ref-RSWP_2_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSWP_2-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> When asked about wind generation by MP <a href="/wiki/Alan_Whitehead" title="Alan Whitehead">Alan Whitehead</a> at <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister%27s_Questions" title="Prime Minister&#39;s Questions">Prime Minister's Questions</a> on 26 October, Sunak responded that, as outlined in the Conservative manifesto of 2019, he would focus on long term energy security, including more offshore wind.<sup id="cite_ref-RSWP_3_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSWP_3-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> Onshore wind generation was made difficult by the National Planning Policy Framework 2016 Update, but as part of his predecessors' policies, the planning laws were set to be relaxed.<sup id="cite_ref-RSWP_4_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSWP_4-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy_2">Foreign policy</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Joe_Biden,_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego,_California,_March_13,_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0188.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/President_Joe_Biden%2C_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego%2C_California%2C_March_13%2C_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0188.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/President_Joe_Biden%2C_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego%2C_California%2C_March_13%2C_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0188.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/President_Joe_Biden%2C_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego%2C_California%2C_March_13%2C_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0188.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6173" data-file-height="4115" /></a><figcaption>Sunak, Joe Biden and Australian prime minister <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Albanese" title="Anthony Albanese">Anthony Albanese</a> at the <a href="/wiki/AUKUS" title="AUKUS">AUKUS</a> meeting in San Diego, California, 13 March 2023</figcaption></figure>
<p>In July 2022, during his run for the Conservative Party leadership, he called China the "biggest long-term threat" to the UK, adding that "They torture, detain and indoctrinate their own people, including in <a href="/wiki/Xinjiang" title="Xinjiang">Xinjiang</a> and Hong Kong, in contravention of their human rights. And they have continually rigged the global economy in their favour by suppressing their currency." He accused China of supporting Russian president <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> and that it was "stealing our technology and infiltrating our universities".<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Sunak softened his attitude after becoming prime minister, calling the country a "systemic challenge" instead of a "threat", and that the West would "manage this sharpening competition, including with diplomacy and engagement".<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sunak described Saudi Arabia as a "partner" and "ally", but said that the British government does not ignore <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia" title="Human rights in Saudi Arabia">human rights violations in Saudi Arabia</a>. According to Sunak, "It's absolutely right that" the British government "engages with our partners and allies around the world as we contemplate how best to ensure energy security for this country."<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> During his chancellorship, Sunak also opposed US president Joe Biden's plan to introduce a minimum 21 percent global business tax.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_133-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, Sunak supported the <a href="/wiki/Status_of_Jerusalem" title="Status of Jerusalem">recognition of Jerusalem</a> as the capital of Israel.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Russia_and_Ukraine">Russia and Ukraine</span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._(52509631592).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._%2852509631592%29.jpg/220px-The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._%2852509631592%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._%2852509631592%29.jpg/330px-The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._%2852509631592%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._%2852509631592%29.jpg/440px-The_President_of_Ukraine_met_with_the_British_Prime_Minister_in_Kyiv._%2852509631592%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2001" /></a><figcaption>Sunak with Ukrainian president <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy" title="Volodymyr Zelenskyy">Volodymyr Zelenskyy</a> during his first visit to <a href="/wiki/Kyiv" title="Kyiv">Kyiv</a> in November 2022</figcaption></figure>
<p>In regards to the <a href="/wiki/Russo%E2%80%93Ukraine_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Russo–Ukraine War">Russo–Ukraine War</a> and the <a href="/wiki/2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine">2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine</a>, Sunak supports Ukraine and economic sanctions against Russia but opposes British military intervention in Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-:42_132-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:42-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> After meeting Ukrainian president <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy" title="Volodymyr Zelenskyy">Volodymyr Zelenskyy</a> during his first visit to <a href="/wiki/Kyiv" title="Kyiv">Kyiv</a> in November 2022, Sunak said: "I am proud of how the UK stood with Ukraine from the very beginning. And I am here today to say the UK and our allies will continue to stand with Ukraine, as it fights to end this barbarous war and deliver a just peace. While Ukraine's armed forces succeed in pushing back Russian forces on the ground, civilians are being brutally bombarded from the air. We are today providing new air defence, including anti-aircraft guns, radar and anti-drone equipment, and stepping up humanitarian support for the cold, hard winter ahead."<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="LGBT_rights">LGBT rights</span></h3>
<p>In July 2022, Sunak said that he wanted the UK to be "the safest and greatest country in the world to be LGBT+". When asked about alleged or perceived <a href="/wiki/Transphobia" title="Transphobia">transphobia</a> within his party, he stated that "prejudice against trans people is wrong. The Conservative Party is an open, welcoming family to everybody across society, no matter who they are and irrespective of their background."<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> He has stated he believes that <a href="/wiki/Transgender" title="Transgender">transgender</a> people should be "respected" but has implied that he did not believe that women can have penises.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> He has said that he views biology as "important" and "fundamental" regarding <a href="/wiki/Bathroom_bill" title="Bathroom bill">bathrooms</a> and <a href="/wiki/Transgender_people_in_sports" title="Transgender people in sports">competitive sports</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_133-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Immigration">Immigration</span></h3>
<p>Sunak has expressed support for lowering <a href="/wiki/Net_migration" class="mw-redirect" title="Net migration">net migration</a>. An official spokesperson said Sunak was "committed to ensuring we have control over our borders and the public rightly expects us to control immigration and have a system that works best for the UK."<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> He has said that the "current asylum system is broken and it needs to be fixed urgently", saying he would, in his first 100 days as prime minister, "tighten our statutory definition of who qualifies for asylum in the UK ... This will prevent anyone who enters the UK illegally from staying here", that the "Parliament will be given control of the number of refugees we accept each year", that he "cannot underestimate the role of data sharing which will make it easier to identify people who are in the UK illegally", and that the <a href="/wiki/Rwanda_asylum_plan" title="Rwanda asylum plan">Rwanda asylum plan</a> is "the right one".<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> Responding to criticism surrounding some of his proposals about illegal immigration, Sunak said there was "absolutely nothing racist" about it.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> On 4 January 2023, Sunak set out his priorities for 2023, which included: "We will pass new laws to stop small boats, making sure that if you come to this country illegally, you are detained and swiftly removed."<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_image">Public image</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg/220px-Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg/330px-Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg/440px-Levelling_up_-_Sunak_and_Hunt.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5664" data-file-height="3815" /></a><figcaption>Sunak and <a href="/wiki/Jeremy_Hunt" title="Jeremy Hunt">Jeremy Hunt</a> holding a "<a href="/wiki/Levelling-up_policy_of_the_British_government" title="Levelling-up policy of the British government">Levelling Up</a>" sign</figcaption></figure>
<p>Following his appointment as chancellor, Sunak arrived in public discourse from relative obscurity.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_180-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> In the early stages of the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="COVID-19 pandemic in the United Kingdom">COVID-19 pandemic</a>, he was popular by the standards of British politics, described by one analyst as having "better ratings than any politician since the heydays of <a href="/wiki/Tony_Blair" title="Tony Blair">Tony Blair</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-:1_181-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> Various polls showed Sunak remained overwhelmingly popular among Conservative supporters and many other Britons throughout 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In an <a href="/wiki/Ipsos_MORI" title="Ipsos MORI">Ipsos MORI</a> poll in September 2020, Sunak had the highest satisfaction score of any British Chancellor since Labour's <a href="/wiki/Denis_Healey" title="Denis Healey">Denis Healey</a> in April 1978, and was widely seen as <a href="/wiki/Leadership_approval_opinion_polling_for_the_next_United_Kingdom_general_election#Hypothetical_polling" title="Leadership approval opinion polling for the next United Kingdom general election">the favourite to become the next Prime Minister</a> and leader of Conservative Party after Boris Johnson.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> Sunak developed a <a href="/wiki/Cult_following" title="Cult following">cult media following</a>, with jokes and gossip about his attractiveness widespread on social media and in magazines, gaining the nickname "Dishi Rishi".<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_180-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:12_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Public attitudes towards Sunak remained broadly positive in 2021,<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> though his popularity declined steadily over time.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_181-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> By early 2022, with the cost of living becoming a growing focus of public concern, Sunak's response as chancellor was perceived as inadequate and he received some of his lowest approval ratings, which continued as the Sunak family's financial affairs came under scrutiny.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> By the time he resigned as chancellor in July 2022, Sunak's approval ratings slightly recovered.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> In October 2022, following his appointment as prime minister, Sunak's personal favourability ratings increased.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> By July 2023, Sunak's approval ratings had decreased back to a similar level to when he resigned as chancellor.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 20 January 2023, Sunak was issued a fixed penalty notice by <a href="/wiki/Lancashire_Police" class="mw-redirect" title="Lancashire Police">Lancashire Police</a> for failing to wear a seatbelt in the back of his ministerial car while filming an <a href="/wiki/Instagram" title="Instagram">Instagram</a> video to promote his government's <a href="/wiki/Levelling-up_policy_of_the_British_government" title="Levelling-up policy of the British government">levelling up policy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> Sunak apologised for the incident, saying it was an "error of judgement".<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Many, such as the MP for <a href="/wiki/Blackpool_South_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Blackpool South (UK Parliament constituency)">Blackpool South</a>, <a href="/wiki/Scott_Benton_(politician)" title="Scott Benton (politician)">Scott Benton</a>, suggested that the targeting of Sunak by the police was "politically motivated" and was not a "good use of frontline resources". Benton also asserted his belief that Britons would rather crime-fighting efforts were focused elsewhere.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_(geograph_2028063).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_%28geograph_2028063%29.jpg/220px-Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_%28geograph_2028063%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_%28geograph_2028063%29.jpg/330px-Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_%28geograph_2028063%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_%28geograph_2028063%29.jpg/440px-Kirby_Sigston_Manor_and_Lake_%28geograph_2028063%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="960" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Kirby_Sigston_Manor" title="Kirby Sigston Manor">Kirby Sigston Manor</a>, owned by Sunak and Murty</figcaption></figure>
<p>In August 2009, Sunak married <a href="/wiki/Akshata_Murty" title="Akshata Murty">Akshata Murty</a>, the daughter of <a href="/wiki/N._R._Narayana_Murthy" class="mw-redirect" title="N. R. Narayana Murthy">N. R. Narayana Murthy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sudha_Murty" title="Sudha Murty">Sudha Murty</a>. His father-in-law is the founder of the technology company <a href="/wiki/Infosys" title="Infosys">Infosys</a>, in which Murty owns a stake.<sup id="cite_ref-Telegraph_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Telegraph-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> Sunak and Murty met while studying at <a href="/wiki/Stanford_University" title="Stanford University">Stanford University</a> in the US; they have two daughters: Krishna (born 2011) and Anoushka (born 2013).<sup id="cite_ref-Telegraph_10-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Telegraph-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-econindia_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-econindia-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> They own several houses, including <a href="/wiki/Kirby_Sigston_Manor" title="Kirby Sigston Manor">Kirby Sigston Manor</a> in the village of <a href="/wiki/Kirby_Sigston" title="Kirby Sigston">Kirby Sigston</a>, North Yorkshire, a <a href="/wiki/Mews" title="Mews">mews</a> house in <a href="/wiki/Earl%27s_Court" title="Earl&#39;s Court">Earl's Court</a> in central London, a flat on the <a href="/wiki/Old_Brompton_Road" title="Old Brompton Road">Old Brompton Road</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Kensington" title="South Kensington">South Kensington</a>, and a penthouse apartment on <a href="/wiki/Ocean_Avenue_(Santa_Monica)" title="Ocean Avenue (Santa Monica)">Ocean Avenue</a> in <a href="/wiki/Santa_Monica,_California" title="Santa Monica, California">Santa Monica, California</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Edwardes_9-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edwardes-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-observer-20220403_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-observer-20220403-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> In April 2022, it was reported that Sunak and Murty had moved out of the flat above 10 Downing Street to a newly refurbished West London home for domestic reasons.<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> In October 2022, the Sunaks resumed residence of their former official home at 10 Downing Street, this time as prime minister and reversing the trend started in 1997 of prime ministers living in the four bedroom flat above <a href="/wiki/11_Downing_Street" title="11 Downing Street">11 Downing Street</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sunak is a <a href="/wiki/Teetotaller" class="mw-redirect" title="Teetotaller">teetotaller</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Hooker_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hooker-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Business2_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Business2-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> He has stated he is a <a href="/wiki/Coca-Cola" title="Coca-Cola">Coca-Cola</a> addict and now has seven dental fillings due to excessive consumption when he was younger.<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> He was previously a governor of the <a href="/wiki/East_London_Science_School" class="mw-redirect" title="East London Science School">East London Science School</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-PE_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PE-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Sunak has a <a href="/wiki/Labrador_Retriever" title="Labrador Retriever">Labrador</a> called Nova and is a cricket and horse racing enthusiast.<sup id="cite_ref-Roberts_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roberts-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> As chancellor, Sunak rose early for a daily Peloton workout and was a fan of fitness instructor <a href="/wiki/Cody_Rigsby" title="Cody Rigsby">Cody Rigsby</a>. Sunak is a close friend of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Spectator" title="The Spectator">The Spectator</a></i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em;">&#39;s</span> former political editor <a href="/wiki/James_Forsyth_(journalist)" title="James Forsyth (journalist)">James Forsyth</a>, whom he has known since their school days. Sunak was the best man at Forsyth's wedding to the journalist <a href="/wiki/Allegra_Stratton" title="Allegra Stratton">Allegra Stratton</a>, and they are godparents to each other's children.<sup id="cite_ref-Edwardes_9-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edwardes-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> He appointed Forsyth as his political secretary in December 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sunak is a steadfast <a href="/wiki/Southampton_F.C." title="Southampton F.C.">Southampton F.C.</a> fan.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> When asked what his ideal job would be if he was not a politician, he replied that if he could "run Southampton Football Club" he would be a "very happy man".<sup id="cite_ref-The_Independent_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Independent-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sunak is a Hindu and identifies as British Indian, stating that he is "thoroughly British" but with an Indian religious and cultural heritage.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> He took his oath as an MP at the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="House of Commons of the United Kingdom">House of Commons</a> on the <i><a href="/wiki/Bhagavad_Gita" title="Bhagavad Gita">Bhagavad Gita</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Business2_19-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Business2-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_Charles_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Coronation of Charles III">coronation of Charles III</a>, Sunak gave a reading from the New Testament book of <a href="/wiki/Epistle_to_the_Colossians" title="Epistle to the Colossians">Colossians</a> – <a href="https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Bible_(King_James)/Colossians#1:9" class="extiw" title="s:Bible (King James)/Colossians">Colossians 1:9–17</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Murder_of_George_Floyd" title="Murder of George Floyd">murder of George Floyd</a> by a police officer, Sunak said he had also faced racism in his life.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_133-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
